Vaccination in Children with Paediatric Autoimmune and Autoinflammatory Rheumatic Diseases: Evidence on Safety, Immunogenicity and Effectiveness Across Live-Attenuated, mRNA and Recombinant Vaccines

Authors: Mohammad Hamad Said, Infection & Immunity, Immuno/reuma patientenzorg, Joris van Montfrans, Bas Vastert, Marc Jansen, Joost Swart
Original abstract (English)
Vaccination in children with paediatric autoimmune and autoinflammatory rheumatic diseases (pedAIIRDs) is clinically important but challenging. These children have an increased risk of infections due to both their underlying immune-mediated disease and the immunosuppressive therapies required for disease control. At the same time, immunosuppression may reduce vaccine-induced immune responses, while concerns about disease flares and the safety of live-attenuated vaccines may result in delayed or incomplete vaccination. This thesis aimed to generate clinically applicable, real-world evidence on the safety, immunogenicity and effectiveness of vaccination in children with pedAIIRDs receiving immunosuppressive therapy. Five studies addressed three vaccine platforms: the live-attenuated measles-mumps-rubella (MMR) booster vaccine, COVID-19 mRNA vaccines and the recombinant zoster vaccine (RZV; Shingrix). In a cohort of 186 children with juvenile idiopathic arthritis (JIA), MMR booster vaccination was not associated with increased disease activity, including in children receiving biological disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs, and no serious vaccine-related adverse events were observed. Long-term evaluation of 182 children with JIA showed that most patients maintained protective antibodies against measles, mumps and rubella. However, measles seroprotection five years after vaccination was significantly lower in patients receiving biologic therapy at vaccination than in those not receiving biologics, supporting a targeted approach to serological monitoring. A systematic review and meta-analysis demonstrated that COVID-19 vaccination was associated with a marked reduction in the risk of multisystem inflammatory syndrome in children (MIS-C). In a prospective cohort of 157 children with paediatric rheumatic diseases, COVID-19 mRNA vaccination induced humoral responses in nearly all patients and adequate T-cell responses in most tested patients. Although antibody concentrations were lower in patients receiving biologic therapy, vaccine effectiveness against symptomatic COVID-19 was preserved. No increase in disease activity, myocarditis or serious adverse events was identified. Finally, off-label use of RZV was evaluated in ten varicella-zoster-virus-naïve immunocompromised children for whom live-attenuated varicella vaccination was unsuitable. Nine of ten patients seroconverted following two doses. Two subsequently developed mild breakthrough varicella after household exposure and recovered without hospitalisation or antiviral treatment. Together, these studies support a shift from broad vaccine avoidance towards individualised, treatment-specific vaccination strategies in immunocompromised children. The findings provide evidence for the safe use of selected live vaccines, targeted monitoring of long-term vaccine-induced immunity, effective use of mRNA vaccines and further evaluation of non-live vaccine alternatives. These results may contribute to future national and international vaccination recommendations for children with immune-mediated inflammatory diseases.

传染病流行病学的数学建模:理论基础、结构类型学和公共卫生应用的系统范围审查(预印本)

Mathematical Modeling in Infectious Disease Epidemiology: A Systematic Scoping Review of Theoretical Foundations, Structural Typologies, and Applications for Global Public Health (Preprint)
作者： José Daniel Sánchez
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在综合数学模型在传染病流行病学中的理论基础、结构类型和公共卫生应用,解决该主题文献的碎片化问题。
设计与对象
我们按照Arksey和O'Malley框架和Joanna Briggs Institute指南进行了系统范围审查,搜索了六个电子数据库,涵盖了1991年1月至2025年12月期间发表的文献。
核心方法
审查确定并系统比较了六种结构模型家族:确定性 compartimental、随机、基于网络、基于代理、统计/混合和分数阶。关键流行病学参数包括基本再生数(R0)、有效再生数R(t)、病死率(CFR)和潜伏期和潜伏期。
主要结果
R0估计值范围从2009年大流行流感H1N1的1.4-1.6到未接种疫苗人群中的麻疹的12-18。应用范围包括结核病、艾滋病、SARS、MERS-CoV、大流行流感、登革热和COVID-19。
解释与创新
审查提出了一种简化的生物行为动态传播率(β(t) = max(0,β0 + αt - θI)),该率考虑了依从性疲劳和患病率-弹性风险意识。它确定了三个关键的方法学空白:早期暴发阶段的验证、人类行为的整合以及模型家族之间的系统比较。
科研与公卫意义
数学模型是传染病流行病学不可或缺的工具,但其最大效用取决于稳健的验证、方法学的透明性和高质量的数据。本审查提供了全面的类型学比较和综合,同时阐述了一个可操作的研究议程,优先考虑标准化验证平台、行为整合和多学科合作,以应对未来的全球健康紧急情况。
局限与证据强度
只有28%的研究提供了代码或数据以确保可重复性。

Mathematical Modeling in Infectious Disease Epidemiology: A Systematic Scoping Review of Theoretical Foundations, Structural Typologies, and Applications for Global Public Health (Preprint)

Authors: José Daniel Sánchez
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Mathematical models have become the intellectual infrastructure of modern infectious disease epidemiology. However, the literature on this topic is highly fragmented across different model families, specific pathogens, and methodological niches, lacking a comprehensive and integrative synthesis. OBJECTIVE This systematic scoping review aims to synthesize the theoretical foundations, structural typology, and public health applications of mathematical models in infectious disease epidemiology, while identifying critical methodological gaps to guide future research and practice. METHODS We conducted a systematic scoping review following the Arksey and O'Malley framework, updated by Levac et al. and the Joanna Briggs Institute guidelines, reported according to the PRISMA extension for Scoping Reviews (PRISMA-ScR). We searched six electronic databases (PubMed/MEDLINE, Embase, Web of Science, Scopus, MathSciNet, and Google Scholar) for literature published between January 1991 and December 2025. From 4,217 initially identified records, 228 were assessed in full text, and 50 studies were included in the final synthesis. RESULTS The review identified and systematically compared six structural model families: deterministic compartmental, stochastic, network-based, agent-based, statistical/hybrid, and fractional-order. Key epidemiological parameters synthesized included the basic reproduction number (R0), the effective reproduction number R(t), the case fatality ratio (CFR), and the latent and incubation periods. Documented R0 estimates ranged from 1.4-1.6 for pandemic influenza H1N1 (2009) to 12-18 for measles in unvaccinated populations. Applications spanned tuberculosis, HIV/AIDS, SARS, MERS-CoV, pandemic influenza, dengue fever, and COVID-19. While 96% of studies transparently described their model structure, only 28% provided code or data for reproducibility. To address a critical gap in behavioral modeling, we propose a parsimonious bio-behavioral dynamic transmission rate (β(t) = max(0, β0 + αt - θI)) that incorporates adherence fatigue and prevalence-elastic risk awareness. The review identified three critical methodological gaps: validation during early outbreak phases, integration of human behavior, and systematic comparisons between model families. CONCLUSIONS Mathematical models are indispensable tools for infectious disease epidemiology, yet their maximum utility depends on robust validation, methodological transparency, and high-quality data. This review provides a comprehensive typological comparison and synthesis of documented parameters while articulating an actionable research agenda prioritizing standardized validation platforms, behavioral integration, and multidisciplinary collaboration for future global health emergencies.

The review provides a comprehensive typological comparison and synthesis of documented parameters while articulating an actionable research agenda prioritizing standardized validation platforms, behavioral integration, and multidisciplinary collaboration for future global health emergencies.

Only 28% of studies provided code or data for reproducibility.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

南非 麻疹 废水和环境 监测 的公众可接受性、生物伦理和法律治理

Public acceptability, bioethics, and legal governance of wastewater and environmental surveillance for measles in South Africa
作者： Rukudzo Matanda, Rochelle H. Holm, Sibonginkosi Maposa, Lebohang Rabotapi, Mukhlid Yousif, Kerrigan McCarthy, Holm, R. H.
摘要中文翻译摘要 目的 本研究调查了南非城市中心关于废水使用和环境 麻疹 的公众意见,涵盖伦理、法律和公共卫生政策主题,并将这些意见映射到现有的生物伦理和法律先例。设计亲自实地调查。设置 南非豪登省的三个大都市集水区(茨瓦内市、艾库胡莱尼市和约翰内斯堡市)。参与者 在选定的下水道集水区居住或工作的 100 名成年社区成员作为样本。主要结果衡量基线废水和环境 监测 意识、特定目标的可接受性、空间监测偏好、数据共享舒适度以及法律/隐私认知。结果 WES 的基线认知度较低(19%)。使用废水和环境 监测 监测一系列生物和化学目标得到了高度支持。然而,可接受性取决于空间尺度:83%的人支持宏观层面的“全市范围”监测,而支持局部抽样(个人家庭和学校)的人则下降至28%。科学和公共卫生机构对数据处理的机构信任度很高。结论 在南非城市,公众对 WES 对传染病(特别是 麻疹)的接受度很高,但取决于宏观层面的数据聚合(“聚合匿名”)和专门科学实体的治理。鉴于社区对 麻疹 监测 的大力支持及其与国家生物伦理和法律框架的一致性,这种 麻疹 方法的生物伦理和法律要求可以得到满足,为针对传染病的废水和环境 监测 的常规实施铺平道路。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
麻疹病毒,历史分类学名称,风疹和 麻疹 疾病。生物相关的动物、分类学、模型、差异-诊断 和比较实体仅是补充性的,除非还存在材料目标特异性证据;只有显式的词汇或不相关的硬排除才是最终的。
证据基础与方法
亲自实地调查。设置 南非豪登省的三个大都市集水区(茨瓦内市、艾库胡莱尼市和约翰内斯堡市)。参与者 在选定的下水道集水区居住或工作的 100 名成年社区成员作为样本。
共识与结论
在南非城市,公众对 WES 对传染病(特别是 麻疹)的接受度很高,但取决于宏观层面的数据聚合(“聚合匿名”)和专门科学实体的治理。
争议与缺口
可接受性取决于空间规模:83% 支持宏观层面的“全市范围”监测,而对局部抽样(个人家庭和学校)的支持率下降至 28%。
科研与实践启示
鉴于社区对 麻疹 监测 的大力支持及其与国家生物伦理和法律框架的一致性,这种 麻疹 方法的生物伦理和法律要求可以得到满足,为针对传染病的废水和环境 监测 的常规实施铺平道路。

Public acceptability, bioethics, and legal governance of wastewater and environmental surveillance for measles in South Africa

Authors: Rukudzo Matanda, Rochelle H. Holm, Sibonginkosi Maposa, Lebohang Rabotapi, Mukhlid Yousif, Kerrigan McCarthy, Holm, R. H.
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Objectives This study investigates public opinion regarding the use of wastewater and environmental surveillance for measles in South African urban centres across ethical, legal, and public health policy themes, and maps these opinions onto existing bioethical and legal precedents. Design In-person field survey. Setting Three metropolitan catchment areas in Gauteng Province, South Africa (City of Tshwane, City of Ekurhuleni, and City of Johannesburg). Participants A sample of 100 adult community members residing or working within the selected sewer catchment areas. Primary outcome measures Baseline wastewater and environmental surveillance awareness, target-specific acceptability, spatial monitoring preferences, data-sharing comfort, and legal/privacy perceptions. Results Baseline awareness of WES was low (19%). Support was high for monitoring a range of biological and chemical targets using wastewater and environmental surveillance. However, acceptability was dependent on spatial scale: 83% supported macro-level "city-wide" monitoring, whereas support fell to 28% for localized sampling (individual homes and schools). Institutional trust in data handling was high among scientific and public health bodies. Conclusions Public acceptability of WES for communicable diseases, particularly measles, in urban South Africa is high but conditional upon macro-level data aggregation ("anonymity by aggregation") and governance by dedicated scientific entities. Given strong community support for measles surveillance and its alignment with the country’s bioethical and legal frameworks, the bioethical and legal requirements for this surveillance approach can be satisfied, paving the way for routine implementation of wastewater and environmental surveillance for communicable diseases.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Measles virus, historical taxonomic names, rubeola and measles disease. Biologically related animal, taxonomic, model, differential-diagnosis and comparative entities are supplementary-only unless material target-specific evidence is also present; only explicit lexical or unrelated hard exclusions are terminal.
Evidence base and method
In-person field survey. Setting Three metropolitan catchment areas in Gauteng Province, South Africa (City of Tshwane, City of Ekurhuleni, and City of Johannesburg). Participants A sample of 100 adult community members residing or working within the selected sewer catchment areas.
Consensus and conclusions
Public acceptability of WES for communicable diseases, particularly measles, in urban South Africa is high but conditional upon macro-level data aggregation ("anonymity by aggregation") and governance by dedicated scientific entities.
Controversies and gaps
Acceptability was dependent on spatial scale: 83% supported macro-level "city-wide" monitoring, whereas support fell to 28% for localized sampling (individual homes and schools).
Research and practice implications
Given strong community support for measles surveillance and its alignment with the country’s bioethical and legal frameworks, the bioethical and legal requirements for this surveillance approach can be satisfied, paving the way for routine implementation of wastewater and environmental surveillance for communicable diseases.

公共卫生部门对安大略省 2024-2025 年 麻疹 暴发 中的挑战、障碍和经验教训的看法:混合方法调查。

Public health unit perspectives on challenges, barriers, and lessons learned from Ontario's 2024-2025 measles outbreak: a mixed-methods survey.
作者： Catharine Chambers, Janice Sarmiento, Megan Nguyen-Orlando, Elizabeth M. Brown, Tara Harris, Reed Morrison, Christine Navarro, Sarah E. Wilson, Jeffrey M. Pernica, Jessica P. Hopkins
摘要中文翻译目标:系统地收集公共卫生单位 (PHU) 对安大略省多司法管辖区 麻疹 暴发(2024 年 10 月至 2025 年 10 月)的挑战、障碍和经验教训的看法。方法:我们于 2025 年 6 月/7 月进行了一项混合方法调查。我们邀请参与 麻疹 暴发 和卫生部的 PHU 工作人员回答在线参与调查。我们分析了多项选择并使用计数和比例对问题进行了排名。对于开放式问题,我们进行了归纳主题分析,以确定共同主题。结果:我们纳入了 26 份调查回复(回复率为 83%)。截至调查时,大约三分之二 (68%) 的参与 PHU 自 2024 年 10 月以来报告了至少一例 暴发 相关病例。受访者认为,缺乏全面的免疫登记处 (69%)、免疫覆盖率低于群体免疫阈值 (39%)、工作量需求或竞争优先事项 (39%) 以及排除学校或工作环境中的易感接触者 (39%) 是他们的主要挑战/障碍。对于准备和应对活动,受访者认为增加人力资源能力(63%)和使用技术工具(38%)进行病例和接触者管理是最有影响力的策略,同时执行或扩大省级学校免疫立法(42%)。吸取的经验教训集中在及时有效的 暴发 响应、支持准备、现代化数据系统和采用技术创新、标准化和简化协议、社区参与和关系建设以及战略沟通。结论:需要进一步开展公共卫生工作,以继续加强安大略省的免疫接种、监测 和 暴发 的准备和应对行动。这些也将有助于加拿大重新获得 麻疹 消除地位。

范围与问题
系统地收集公共卫生部门 (PHU) 对安大略省多辖区 麻疹 暴发(2024 年 10 月至 2025 年 10 月)的挑战、障碍和经验教训的看法。
证据基础与方法
我们于 2025 年 6 月/7 月进行了一项混合方法调查。
共识与结论
截至调查时,约三分之二 (68%) 的参与 PHU 自 2024 年 10 月以来至少报告了 1 起与 暴发 相关的病例。
争议与缺口
受访者认为,缺乏全面的免疫登记(69%)、免疫覆盖率低于群体免疫阈值(39%)、工作量需求或竞争优先事项(39%)以及排除学校或工作环境中的易感接触者(39%)是他们面临的主要挑战/障碍。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步开展公共卫生工作,以继续加强安大略省的免疫、监测 和 暴发 的准备和应对行动。

Public health unit perspectives on challenges, barriers, and lessons learned from Ontario's 2024-2025 measles outbreak: a mixed-methods survey.

Authors: Catharine Chambers, Janice Sarmiento, Megan Nguyen-Orlando, Elizabeth M. Brown, Tara Harris, Reed Morrison, Christine Navarro, Sarah E. Wilson, Jeffrey M. Pernica, Jessica P. Hopkins
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVE: To systematically gather perspectives from public health units (PHUs) on the challenges, barriers, and lessons learned from the multi-jurisdictional measles outbreak in Ontario (October 2024-October 2025). METHODS: We conducted a mixed-methods survey in June/July 2025. We invited PHU staff involved with the measles outbreak and the Ministry of Health to respond to an online engagement survey. We analyzed multi-choice and ranked questions using counts and proportions. For open-ended questions, we conducted an inductive thematic analysis to identify common themes. RESULTS: We included 26 survey responses (83% response rate). At the time of the survey, about two-thirds (68%) of participating PHUs had at least one outbreak-associated case reported since October 2024. Respondents identified the lack of a comprehensive immunization registry (69%), immunization coverage below herd immunity thresholds (39%), workload demands or competing priorities (39%), and excluding susceptible contacts from school or work settings (39%) as their main challenges/barriers. For preparedness and response activities, respondents identified increased human resources capacity (63%) and use of technological tools (38%) for case and contact management as their most impactful strategies, along with enforcement or expansion of provincial school immunization legislation (42%). Lessons learned focused on timely and efficient outbreak response, supporting readiness, modernizing data systems and adopting technological innovations, standardizing and streamlining protocols, community engagement and relationship building, and strategic communications. CONCLUSION: Further public health efforts will be needed to continue to strengthen Ontario's immunization, surveillance, and outbreak preparedness and response actions. These will also contribute to Canada regaining its measles elimination status.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
To systematically gather perspectives from public health units (PHUs) on the challenges, barriers, and lessons learned from the multi-jurisdictional measles outbreak in Ontario (October 2024-October 2025).
Evidence base and method
We conducted a mixed-methods survey in June/July 2025.
Consensus and conclusions
At the time of the survey, about two-thirds (68%) of participating PHUs had at least one outbreak-associated case reported since October 2024.
Controversies and gaps
Respondents identified the lack of a comprehensive immunization registry (69%), immunization coverage below herd immunity thresholds (39%), workload demands or competing priorities (39%), and excluding susceptible contacts from school or work settings (39%) as their main challenges/barriers.
Research and practice implications
Further public health efforts will be needed to continue to strengthen Ontario's immunization, surveillance, and outbreak preparedness and response actions.

完全和不完全免疫史儿童麻疹临床表现的差异:文献复习

Differences in Clinical Manifestations of Measles in Children with Complete and Incomplete Immunization History: Literature Review
作者： A. Vania Nurul Anisah, Husni Esa Darussalam, Nur Ahmad Tabri
摘要中文翻译麻疹是一种传染性极强的急性传染病。本次评价评估的六种临床表现是发烧、咳嗽、鼻炎、结膜炎、气短和腹泻。本文献综述旨在根据儿童的麻疹免疫完成史或水平来分析儿童这六种表现的差异。通过 PubMed、Google Scholar 和 ScienceDirect 进行搜索,优先考虑 2020 年至 2026 年的原创研究。印度尼西亚语、英语或其他语言的文章均包含全文且可充分摘录。叙述和比较综合中总共纳入了五项儿科研究。结果表明,发烧是所有群体中最常见的症状,从而限制了临床区分免疫状态的能力。在一些研究中,接种疫苗的儿童的咳嗽、鼻炎和腹泻往往较少,而有关结膜炎的研究结果却不一致。关于呼吸短促的直接证据有限,因为大多数研究报告了间接呼吸指标。总之,麻疹 免疫史往往与某些结果中较轻的表现相关,但不排除 麻疹 的可能性。诊断仍需要临床评估、流行病学史和实验室确认。

Differences in Clinical Manifestations of Measles in Children with Complete and Incomplete Immunization History: Literature Review

Authors: A. Vania Nurul Anisah, Husni Esa Darussalam, Nur Ahmad Tabri
Original abstract (English)
Measles is a highly contagious acute infectious disease. The six clinical manifestations assessed in this review are fever, cough, coryza, conjunctivitis, shortness of breath, and diarrhea. This literature review aims to analyze differences in these six manifestations in children based on their history or level of measles immunization completion. A search was conducted through PubMed, Google Scholar, and ScienceDirect, prioritizing original studies from 2020–2026. Articles in Indonesian, English, or other languages that were available in full text and could be adequately extracted were included. A total of five pediatric studies were included in the narrative and comparative synthesis. The results showed that fever was the most common symptom across all groups, thus limiting the ability to clinically differentiate immunization status. Cough, coryza, and diarrhea tended to be lower in vaccinated children in some studies, while findings regarding conjunctivitis were inconsistent. Direct evidence regarding shortness of breath is limited because most studies reported indirect respiratory indicators. In conclusion, a history of measles immunization tends to be associated with milder manifestations in some outcomes, but does not exclude the possibility of measles. Diagnosis still requires clinical assessment, epidemiological history, and laboratory confirmation.

Consensus and conclusions

Controversies and gaps
Direct evidence regarding shortness of breath is limited because most studies reported indirect respiratory indicators.
Research and practice implications
Diagnosis still requires clinical assessment, epidemiological history, and laboratory confirmation.

Antiviral Therapy for Measles: Progress, Challenges and Perspectives

Antiviral Therapy for Measles: Progress, Challenges and Perspectives
作者： Soukaina Ouannass, Walid Belhaimer, Mahamadou Moustapha Sani Daouda, Djibrill Garba Kalla, Sadia Tina, Fatima Azzahra Lahlou, Samy S. Iskandar, Meryam El Jemli, Lamia Slimani, Moulay Mustapha Ennaji
摘要中文翻译简介/目的 尽管存在有效的 疫苗,但 麻疹 仍然是全球儿科发病率和 死亡率 的主要原因。本综述旨在通过检查当前候选药物、其分子靶点、可用的临床前和临床数据以及治疗应用的关键挑战,评估 麻疹 的特定 抗病毒药物 治疗 的开发进展。方法 使用 PubMed 和官方数据库(WHO、CDC)进行广泛的文献检索(2000-2026 年),包括体外和体内研究(动物模型)、临床试验和国际指南。主要药物包括利巴韦林、法匹拉韦、瑞德西韦、ERDRP-0519、GHP-88309、融合肽抑制剂和单克隆抗体。结果尚未有抗病毒药物专门用于麻疹的批准;然而,一些候选人表现出了希望。利巴韦林是一种广谱 麻疹病毒,已证明对 麻疹病毒 具有体外抑制作用,并且在严重病例(特别是免疫功能低下患者)中具有临床益处,尽管数据仍然有限。ERDRP-0519 及其衍生物 GHP-88309 等新型聚合酶抑制剂已在动物模型中显示出有效的暴露后保护作用。最初为其他病毒开发的核苷类似物(法匹拉韦、瑞德西韦)表现出体外抗 麻疹 活性。针对病毒融合蛋白的肽和单克隆抗体也显示出令人鼓舞的结果。讨论 抗病毒药物 疗法可以改善严重或暴露后病例的管理,特别是对于未接种疫苗的个体。结论 虽然 疫苗接种 仍然是预防的基石,但 抗病毒药物 治疗可以改善严重或暴露后病例的管理,特别是对于未接种疫苗的个体。需要进行临床试验来验证这些策略,并考虑将其纳入未来的全球 麻疹 消除计划。

本综述旨在通过检查当前候选药物、其分子靶点、可用的临床前和临床数据以及治疗应用的关键挑战,评估 麻疹 的特定 抗病毒药物 治疗 的开发进展。
证据基础与方法
主要药物包括利巴韦林、法匹拉韦、瑞德西韦、ERDRP-0519、GHP-88309、融合肽抑制剂和单克隆抗体。
共识与结论
ERDRP-0519 及其衍生物 GHP-88309 等新型聚合酶抑制剂已在动物模型中显示出有效的暴露后保护作用。
争议与缺口
讨论 抗病毒药物 疗法可以改善严重或暴露后病例的管理,特别是对于未接种疫苗的个体。
科研与实践启示
结论 虽然 疫苗接种 仍然是预防的基石,但 抗病毒药物 治疗可以改善严重或暴露后病例的管理,特别是对于未接种疫苗的个体。

Antiviral Therapy for Measles: Progress, Challenges and Perspectives

Authors: Soukaina Ouannass, Walid Belhaimer, Mahamadou Moustapha Sani Daouda, Djibrill Garba Kalla, Sadia Tina, Fatima Azzahra Lahlou, Samy S. Iskandar, Meryam El Jemli, Lamia Slimani, Moulay Mustapha Ennaji
Original abstract (English)
Introduction/Objective Despite the availability of an effective vaccine, measles remains a major cause of pediatric morbidity and mortality worldwide. This review aims to evaluate advances toward the development of specific antiviral treatments for measles by examining current candidates, their molecular targets, available preclinical and clinical data, and key challenges to therapeutic application. Methods An extensive literature search (2000-2026) was conducted using PubMed and official databases (WHO, CDC), including in vitro and in vivo studies (animal models), clinical trials, and international guidelines. The main agents included were ribavirin, favipiravir, remdesivir, ERDRP-0519, GHP-88309, fusion peptide inhibitors, and monoclonal antibodies. Results No antiviral is yet approved specifically for measles; however, several candidates show promise. Ribavirin, a broad-spectrum antiviral, has demonstrated in vitro inhibition of measles virus and clinical benefit in severe cases (especially in immunocompromised patients), although data remain limited. Novel polymerase inhibitors such as ERDRP-0519 and its derivative GHP-88309 have shown effective post-exposure protection in animal models. Nucleoside analogs initially developed for other viruses (favipiravir, remdesivir) exhibit in vitro anti-measles activity. Peptides and monoclonal antibodies targeting the viral fusion protein also demonstrate encouraging results. Discussion Antiviral therapy may improve the management of severe or post-exposure cases, especially in non-vaccinable individuals. Conclusion While vaccination remains the cornerstone of prevention, antiviral therapy may improve the management of severe or post-exposure cases, especially in non-vaccinable individuals. Clinical trials are needed to validate these strategies and consider their integration into future global measles elimination programs.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review aims to evaluate advances toward the development of specific antiviral treatments for measles by examining current candidates, their molecular targets, available preclinical and clinical data, and key challenges to therapeutic application.
Evidence base and method
The main agents included were ribavirin, favipiravir, remdesivir, ERDRP-0519, GHP-88309, fusion peptide inhibitors, and monoclonal antibodies.
Consensus and conclusions
Novel polymerase inhibitors such as ERDRP-0519 and its derivative GHP-88309 have shown effective post-exposure protection in animal models.
Controversies and gaps
Discussion Antiviral therapy may improve the management of severe or post-exposure cases, especially in non-vaccinable individuals.
Research and practice implications
Conclusion While vaccination remains the cornerstone of prevention, antiviral therapy may improve the management of severe or post-exposure cases, especially in non-vaccinable individuals.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

系统综述去重工具的可重复性和有效性失败:在儿科神经放射学语料库上的评估。

Reproducibility and validity failures in systematic review deduplication tools: evaluation on a paediatric neuroradiology corpus.
作者： Lakshmi Vennela Chowdary Kaza, Pallavi S R, Rayya Naffa', Shyamu Patel, Sivakumar Manickam, S R P
摘要中文翻译背景:重复数据删除通常被视为系统评价中的常规预处理步骤,但此阶段引入的错误在系统评价和元分析的首选报告项目 (PRISMA) 流程图中不可见,并且可以通过证据合成传播。目的:评估儿科神经放射学系统评价中常用重复数据删除工具的有效性和可重复性。材料和方法:在 PubMed 和 Embase 中检索了有关儿科 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 神经影像学的同行评审文献,共获得 603 条记录。两位审稿人使用 Zotero(版本 8.0.4)和 Rayyan(2026 年 1 月 21 日更新)独立对合并语料库进行了重复数据删除。通过双审稿人使用标题、出版年份、作者列表和数字对象标识符 (DOI) 对潜在的重复对进行裁决,建立了手动参考标准。将工具输出与该参考标准进行比较,以了解假阳性和假阴性决策以及运行的可重复性。结果:人工裁决确定了 120 条真实重复记录,留下 483 条唯一记录。Zotero 在审阅者之间产生了相同的输出,表明了确定性行为,但产生了 16 个假阳性重复决策和 5 个假阴性,主要与 DOI 冲突有关。Rayyan 发现了更多真实的重复项,但在独立运行中产生了不同的输出,每次运行有 16-17 个误报和 1 个误报。结论:常用的重复数据删除工具可能会在筛选开始之前引入确定性偏差或非确定性记录丢失。在儿科放射学证据合成中,应明确报告重复数据删除软件版本和验证策略。在删除记录之前应考虑可审计的二次检查。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注 麻疹病毒(人麻疹病毒)、风疹和 麻疹 疾病等历史名称,以及 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 等相关病症。核心问题是系统评价中重复数据删除工具的有效性和可重复性,特别是在儿科神经放射学背景下。
证据基础与方法
该综述使用儿科神经放射学语料库评估了重复数据删除工具。检索了 PubMed 和 Embase 中有关儿科 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 神经影像学的文献,结果有 603 条记录。两名审阅者使用 Zotero(版本 8.0.4)和 Rayyan(2026 年 1 月 21 日更新)独立对记录进行重复数据删除。通过双审稿人根据标题、出版年份、作者列表和 DOI 对潜在的重复对进行裁决,建立了手动参考标准。
共识与结论
人工裁定识别出120条真正的重复记录,剩余483条唯一记录。Zotero表现出确定性行为,但有16条假阳性与5条假阴性判断,主要由于DOI冲突。Rayyan识别出更多真正的重复记录,但不同运行之间的输出不一致,每次运行有16-17条假阳性与1条假阴性。综述得出结论,去重工具可能在筛选开始前引入确定性偏差或非确定性记录丢失。
争议与缺口
关于重复数据删除工具的可靠性存在争议,因为它们可能会产生不一致或有偏差的结果。该审查强调了在儿科放射学证据合成中明确报告重复数据删除软件版本和验证策略的必要性。证据还指出了这些工具在再现性和有效性方面的差距,特别是在处理 DOI 冲突和确保运行中的一致输出方面。
科研与实践启示
审查建议在删除记录之前应考虑进行可审计的二次检查。它强调报告重复数据删除方法和软件版本的透明度的重要性,以确保系统审查的可重复性和有效性。此外,它还强调需要进一步研究提高重复数据删除工具的准确性,特别是在处理 DOI 冲突等复杂情况时。

Reproducibility and validity failures in systematic review deduplication tools: evaluation on a paediatric neuroradiology corpus.

Authors: Lakshmi Vennela Chowdary Kaza, Pallavi S R, Rayya Naffa', Shyamu Patel, Sivakumar Manickam, S R P
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Deduplication is usually treated as a routine preprocessing step in systematic reviews, yet errors introduced at this stage are not visible in Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic reviews and Meta-Analyses (PRISMA) flow diagrams and can propagate through evidence synthesis. OBJECTIVE: To evaluate the validity and reproducibility of commonly used deduplication tools in a paediatric neuroradiology systematic review. MATERIALS AND METHODS: PubMed and Embase were searched for peer-reviewed literature on paediatric subacute sclerosing panencephalitis neuroimaging, yielding 603 records. Two reviewers independently deduplicated the combined corpus using Zotero (version 8.0.4) and Rayyan (updated 21 January 2026). A manual reference standard was established by dual-reviewer adjudication of potential duplicate pairs using title, publication year, author list, and Digital Object Identifier (DOI). Tool outputs were compared against this reference standard for false-positive and false-negative decisions and for reproducibility across runs. RESULTS: Manual adjudication identified 120 true duplicate records, leaving 483 unique records. Zotero produced identical outputs across reviewers, indicating deterministic behaviour, but generated 16 false-positive duplicate decisions and 5 false negatives, predominantly related to DOI collision. Rayyan identified more true duplicates but produced non-identical outputs across independent runs, with 16-17 false positives and 1 false negative per run. CONCLUSION: Commonly used deduplication tools can introduce either deterministic bias or non-deterministic record loss before screening begins. In paediatric radiology evidence synthesis, the deduplication software version and verification strategy should be explicitly reported. Auditable secondary checks should be considered before records are removed.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on measles virus (Morbillivirus hominis), historical names like rubeola and measles disease, and related conditions such as subacute sclerosing panencephalitis. The central question is about the validity and reproducibility of deduplication tools in systematic reviews, particularly in the context of paediatric neuroradiology.
Evidence base and method
The review evaluated deduplication tools using a paediatric neuroradiology corpus. PubMed and Embase were searched for literature on paediatric subacute sclerosing panencephalitis neuroimaging, resulting in 603 records. Two reviewers independently deduplicated the records using Zotero (version 8.0.4) and Rayyan (updated 21 January 2026). A manual reference standard was established by dual-reviewer adjudication of potential duplicate pairs based on title, publication year, author list, and DOI.
Consensus and conclusions
The manual adjudication identified 120 true duplicate records, leaving 483 unique records. Zotero showed deterministic behavior but had 16 false-positive and 5 false-negative decisions, mainly due to DOI collisions. Rayyan identified more true duplicates but had non-identical outputs across runs, with 16-17 false positives and 1 false negative per run. The review concludes that deduplication tools can introduce either deterministic bias or non-deterministic record loss before screening begins.
Controversies and gaps
There is controversy regarding the reliability of deduplication tools, as they can produce inconsistent or biased results. The review highlights the need for explicit reporting of deduplication software versions and verification strategies in paediatric radiology evidence synthesis. The evidence also points to gaps in the reproducibility and validity of these tools, particularly in handling DOI collisions and ensuring consistent outputs across runs.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that auditable secondary checks should be considered before removing records. It emphasizes the importance of transparency in reporting deduplication methods and software versions to ensure reproducibility and validity in systematic reviews. Additionally, it highlights the need for further research into improving the accuracy of deduplication tools, especially in handling complex cases like DOI collisions.

索马里援助驱动的卫生体系中的空壳综合征与儿童营养不良危机

The empty shell syndrome in Somalia’s aid‐driven health system and the pediatric malnutrition crisis
作者： Walid Abdulkadir Osman, Khalid Osman, Hassan Muse Ahmed
摘要中文翻译摘要 2026 年初,索马里儿童营养不良达到了临界点,入院人数创五年来新高。由于国际资金削减和冲突导致的燃料成本急剧上升,近 500 家营养诊所同时关闭,这从根本上切断了农村和流离失所人口的医疗保健服务,加剧了这种激增。这一视角审视了索马里援助驱动的卫生系统的“空壳”综合症,该综合症源于对预算外垂直规划的依赖。我们分析了严重急性营养不良 (SAM) 与 麻疹 和霍乱等可预防传染病相互作用产生的综合 死亡率 率。解决在很大程度上被忽视的儿童生存紧急情况需要进行战略转变,从紧急救助转向全面的卫生系统整合。我们建议采取紧急干预措施,包括交通补贴和下放稳定中心,作为拯救生命的立即措施。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦于索马里援助驱动的卫生体系对儿童营养不良危机的影响,特别是严重急性营养不良(SAM)激增及其与可预防传染病如麻疹的相互作用。核心问题是:卫生体系的结构性弱点如何加剧与营养不良相关的死亡率。
证据基础与方法
本综述基于论文的摘要层面证据,讨论了索马里卫生体系的历史和现状背景、国际资金削减的作用以及燃料价格上涨对医疗服务可及性的影响。方法涉及对卫生体系结构及其对儿童生存影响的叙述性分析。
共识与结论
综述得出结论:索马里援助驱动的卫生体系,其特点是垂直化项目和依赖非预算资金,已导致近500个营养诊所关闭,加剧了农村和流离失所人群获得医疗服务的困难。这造成了儿童营养不良的严重危机,入院人数达到五年来最高水平。
争议与缺口
综述指出,缺乏对卫生体系的全面整合,以及缺乏应对营养不良危机根本原因的策略。目前尚不清楚卫生体系的结构性弱点如何具体与麻疹及其他可预防疾病相互作用,从而增加死亡率。
科研与实践启示
综述建议需要紧急干预,例如交通补贴和稳定中心的去中心化,以改善医疗服务的可及性。同时强调,应从紧急物资发放转向更加整合的卫生体系方法,以应对儿童生存危机。}}.{

The empty shell syndrome in Somalia’s aid‐driven health system and the pediatric malnutrition crisis

Authors: Walid Abdulkadir Osman, Khalid Osman, Hassan Muse Ahmed
Original abstract (English)
Abstract In early 2026, Somalia reached a critical threshold in pediatric malnutrition, with admissions hitting five-year highs. This surge is exacerbated by the simultaneous closure of nearly 500 nutrition clinics due to international funding cuts and a sharp, conflict-driven rise in fuel costs, which has fundamentally severed healthcare access for rural and displaced populations. This perspective examines the “empty shell” syndrome of Somalia’s aid-driven health system, which stems from reliance on off-budget, vertical programming. We analyze the combined mortality rates resulting from the interaction of severe acute malnutrition (SAM) with preventable infectious diseases such as measles and cholera. Addressing the largely overlooked child survival emergency requires a strategic shift from emergency handouts to comprehensive health system integration. We propose urgent interventions, including transport subsidies and the decentralization of stabilization centers, as immediate measures to save lives.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the impact of Somalia’s aid-driven health system on the pediatric malnutrition crisis, particularly the surge in severe acute malnutrition (SAM) and its interaction with preventable infectious diseases like measles. The central question is how the structural weaknesses of the health system contribute to the exacerbation of malnutrition-related mortality.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on abstract-level evidence from the paper, which discusses the historical and current context of Somalia’s health system, the role of international funding cuts, and the effect of rising fuel costs on healthcare access. The methodology involves a narrative analysis of the health system’s structure and its implications for child survival.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that the aid-driven health system in Somalia, characterized by vertical programming and reliance on off-budget funding, has led to the closure of nearly 500 nutrition clinics, worsening access to care for rural and displaced populations. This has created a critical situation in pediatric malnutrition, with admissions reaching five-year highs.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the lack of comprehensive integration of health systems and the absence of strategies to address the root causes of the malnutrition crisis. There is a gap in understanding how the structural weaknesses of the health system specifically interact with measles and other preventable diseases to increase mortality.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that urgent interventions, such as transport subsidies and the decentralization of stabilization centers, are needed to improve access to care. It also emphasizes the importance of shifting from emergency handouts to a more integrated health system approach to address the child survival emergency.

How Long Does the Measles Vaccine Last? What to Know About Measles in 2026 - University of Rochester
新闻简报The article from the University of Rochester, published on September 8, 2026, discusses the ongoing measles situation in the United States in 2026, noting a surge in cases in 2025 with 2,289 confirmed infections. It highlights that measles remains highly contagious, with a single infected person potentially infecting up to 90% of non-immune individuals. The article emphasizes the effectiveness of the measles vaccine, stating that two doses provide 97% protection. It also notes that only 88% of children in Monroe County are vaccinated by age two, which is below the 95% needed for herd immunity. The piece advises families to ensure their vaccinations are up to date and to follow guidance from healthcare providers and public health officials to prevent outbreaks. It underscores the importance of vaccination in protecting vulnerable populations, including newborns and those with weakened immune systems, and recommends isolating individuals with suspected measles to reduce transmission.

Skip to newsroom menu Skip to main content Patient Care Education Research Community Skip to main content Newsroom Publications Dialogue NeURoscience Nursing Rochester Medicine Strong Kids Pathology and Laboratory Medicine View All Publications Media Inquiries Images and Video Additional Resources About Us Archives Explore URMC Healthy Living How Long Does the Measles Vaccine Last? What to Know About Measles in 2026 Sep. 8, 2026 Facebook Twitter LinkedIn Email Skip breadcrumb URochester Medicine Home Newsroom Publications Healthy Living How Long Does the Measles Vaccine Last? What to Know About Measles in 2026 After a surge of measles in 2025 with 2,289 confirmed measles cases in the United States, measles circulation remains high in 2026, with over 3,000 cases. Most infections occur in unvaccinated individuals, but what about those who’ve been vaccinated? Here’s what you need to know about MMR vaccine effectiveness, symptoms to watch for, and how to keep your family safe. How Long Does the Measles Vaccine Last? The measles vaccine is very effective, with two doses about 97% effective at preventing the disease if you are exposed to the virus. Most people vaccinated with both measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) doses are protected for life. Even if a vaccinated person does get measles, symptoms are usually much milder, and they’re less likely to spread it to others. Vaccination protects more than just you—it helps prevent the virus from spreading to newborns, people undergoing cancer treatment, and others who can't be vaccinated. Who may need another MMR dose? Adults born between 1963–1967 may need a modernized vaccine. Here’s how you can help: Make sure your family’s vaccinations are up to date Know your vaccine status—ask your healthcare provider Encourage others to get vaccinated Measles Vaccine Effectiveness Before vaccines, nearly every child in the U.S. got measles. Today, the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine is incredibly effective: 1 dose = 93% protection 2 doses = 97% protection What Is the Measles Vaccine Schedule for Children? Dr. Geoff Weinberg, a pediatrician and infectious disease expert at Golisano Children's, provides the latest recommendations for the measles vaccine in children. Children routinely receive two doses of the measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccine: First dose: 12 to 15 months old Second dose: 4 to 6 years old Children who missed these routine vaccinations can still "catch up." They generally need two doses of the MMR vaccine given at least four weeks apart. Confirm with your pediatrician about the appropriate timing. Can Children Receive the MMR Vaccine Early? Children who have received both recommended doses generally do not need another measles vaccination. However, the schedule may change in certain circumstances: International travel: Infants ages 6 to 11 months should receive one dose of MMR before traveling internationally. Children 12 months and older should have two documented doses before international travel. Measles outbreaks: Public health officials may recommend an early dose for infants ages 6 to 11 months who are at increased risk of exposure. Families should follow guidance from their care team and local public health officials. Infants younger than 6 months: The MMR vaccine is not given to this age group. Why are there Measles Outbreaks? Measles is a highly contagious virus that spreads easily through the air. A single infected person can infect up to 90% of non-immune people around them. Vaccination is the best way to protect yourself and your community. “In a population that is not vaccinated, one person can infect another 12 to 18 people. That’s about a 90 percent attack rate and is honestly incredibly scary,” said Jennifer Nayak, MD, associate professor of pediatric infectious diseases with University of Rochester Medicine. Unfortunately, only 88% of children in Monroe County are vaccinated by age two, falling short of the 95% needed for herd immunity. Measles Symptoms to Watch for Measles can be severe—even deadly—especially for young children and people with weakened immune systems. Classic measles symptoms include: High fever Cough and runny nose Red, watery eyes (conjunctivitis) Rash that starts at the hairline and spreads down Measles can also lead to complications such as: Ear infections Pneumonia Rare but fatal brain inflammation occurring either shortly following infection or even years afterwards How Does Measles Spread? Measles is an airborne virus, meaning it spreads through tiny droplets in the air when someone coughs or sneezes. You can catch it just by being in the same room as an infected person, even long after they’ve left. “Measles can stay in the air and the room for up to two hours,” said Dr. Nayak. “That means if you’re going shopping an hour behind someone with measles, you can still potentially get infected. That’s why we’re scared of the contagiousness.” People with measles are contagious for about four days before the rash appears and four days after. That means they can spread the virus even before they know they’re sick. What To Do If You Suspect Measles If you or someone you know has symptoms, it’s essential to act quickly. If you or your child has symptoms like fever and rash, call your doctor before going to any clinic. This allows your provider to deliver care to you in the safest way possible, helping prevent the virus from spreading further, including to infants and others who are unable to be vaccinated. Get Care from Home Virtual Urgent Care is a good choice for common medical conditions and minor illnesses, like colds, coughs, headaches, infections, rashes and more. Check our symptom guide to see if your symptoms can be treated in a video visit. Get Care Now Connect with us! 宾夕法尼亚州卫生部确认624例麻疹病例,涉及37个县-盖茨堡关联

Pennsylvania Department of Health Confirms 624 Measles Cases in 37 Counties - Gettysburg Connection
新闻简报截至2026年9月10日,宾夕法尼亚州卫生部确认了37个县共624例麻疹病例。这些信息是公共卫生响应的一部分,提供了一个仪表板用于追踪病例、住院、死亡、社区传播和疫苗接种努力。该事件被确认为已确认事件,暴发的规模很大,影响了多个县。然而,证据中没有提供关于死亡、住院或社区传播的具体细节。响应措施包括监测和追踪,但没有提到隔离、接触追踪或治疗。信息来源于官方DOH声明,证据被认为可靠且完整。
查看新闻五要素
时间
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部确认624例麻疹病例涉及37个县,时间为2026年9月10日。事件的具体日期在证据中没有明确说明,但信息截至发布日期是当前的。
地点与对象
37个县共报告了624例麻疹病例,没有提供具体的位置或人口细节,仅提供了县一级的信息。
事件
这是一个基于DOH官方声明的已确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
已确认的麻疹病例总数为624例,影响了37个县。证据中没有提供关于死亡、住院或社区传播的具体数字,但提到有仪表板可用于追踪这些指标。风险没有明确说明,但存在已确认病例表明存在公共卫生关注。
应对、状态与不确定性
宾夕法尼亚州卫生部提供了一个仪表板用于追踪麻疹病例、住院、死亡、社区传播和疫苗接种努力。响应是持续的,状态为已确认,没有不确定性或未解决的信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pennsylvania Department of Health Confirms 624 Measles Cases in 37 Counties - Gettysburg Connection

News Brief
The Pennsylvania Department of Health confirmed 624 measles cases across 37 counties as of September 10, 2026. This information is part of an ongoing public health response, with a dashboard available for tracking cases, hospitalizations, deaths, community transmission, and vaccination efforts. The event is reported as confirmed, and the scale of the outbreak is significant, affecting multiple counties. However, specific details about deaths, hospitalizations, or community spread are not provided in the evidence. The response includes monitoring and tracking efforts, but no further actions such as isolation, contact tracing, or treatment are mentioned. The information is sourced from the official DOH statement, and the evidence is considered reliable and complete.

Britannica Editors Last updated Sep. 7, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Quick Summary Table of Contents Table of Contents Quick Summary Ask Anything Top Questions What is measles? Measles is a highly contagious viral disease, a hallmark symptom of which is a blotchy rash. It is most common in children and can be prevented through vaccination. What happens if you get measles? If you get measles, you are likely to develop a high fever, cough, and runny nose and have red, watery eyes. These symptoms are followed by a characteristic rash that spreads from the face downward. Symptoms appear 7–14 days after exposure and can lead to severe complications, including pneumonia, encephalitis (brain inflammation), diarrhea, or death, particularly in people who are unvaccinated. Can you get measles if you had a vaccine? It is possible, though rare, to get measles even after being vaccinated. Such breakthrough infections occur because the MMR vaccine, which protects against measles, is about 97 percent effective after two doses. However, vaccinated individuals who become infected usually experience a milder illness and are less likely to spread it to others, providing crucial protection for vulnerable populations, including the very young and the elderly. Why is measles coming back? Measles is resurging primarily due to declining vaccination rates, which have dropped below the 95 percent threshold required for herd immunity. Increased vaccine hesitancy and interruptions in routine immunization schedules, particularly during the COVID-19 pandemic, have been major contributing factors to this decline. Because the measles virus is highly contagious, it spreads easily and quickly through under-vaccinated communities. What is the recommended treatment for measles? No drug is effective against measles. The only treatment required is control of fever, rest in bed, protection of the eyes, care of the bowels, and sometimes steam inhalations to relieve irritation of the bronchial tree. When no complications occur, the illness lasts 10 days. What are complications of measles? Measles complications are frequent and include superimposed bacterial ear infection, pneumonia, and lung infection. The virus can also cause hepatitis, appendicitis, and gangrene of the extremities. Encephalitis is a rare occurrence. Deaths attributed to measles usually result from secondary bronchopneumonia caused by bacterial organisms entering the inflamed bronchial tree. Can measles affect adults? Measles is most common in children but may appear in older persons who escaped it earlier in life. Immunity to measles following an attack is usually lifelong. Is there a vaccine for measles? Measles vaccines are live vaccines that work against measles alone or in combination with other agents, specifically with rubella (MR), mumps and rubella (MMR), or mumps, rubella, and varicella (MMRV). The vaccines are typically given in two doses, the first before 15 months of age and the second dose later; timing of the dosages varies by country or region. Measles is most common in children but may appear in older persons who escaped it earlier in life. Infants are immune up to four or five months of age if the mother has had the disease. Immunity to measles following an attack is usually lifelong. The disease can be prevented through immunization with the MMR vaccine. Learn more in this list of vaccine-preventable diseases. Transmission and symptoms MeaslesGiant cell in a patient with measles.(more) Measles is so highly communicable that the slightest contact with an active case may infect a susceptible person. After an incubation period of about 10 days, the patient develops fever, redness and watering of the eyes, profuse nasal discharge, and congestion of the mucous membranes of the nose and throat—symptoms often mistaken for those of a severe cold. This period of invasion lasts for 48 to 96 hours. The fever increases with appearance of a blotchy rash, and the temperature may rise as high as 40 °C (about 105 °F) when the rash reaches its maximum. Twenty-four to 36 hours before the rash develops, there appear in the mucous membranes of the mouth typical maculae, called Koplik spots—bluish white specks surrounded by bright red areas about 1/32 inch (0.75 mm) in diameter. After a day or two the rash becomes a deeper red and gradually fades, the temperature drops rapidly, and the catarrhal symptoms disappear. Britannica Quiz A Visit with the Word Doctor: Medical Vocabulary Quiz Treatment and complications No drug is effective against measles. The only treatment required is control of fever, rest in bed, protection of the eyes, care of the bowels, and sometimes steam inhalations to relieve irritation of the bronchial tree. When no complications occur, the illness lasts 10 days. Although uncomplicated measles is seldom fatal, infection with the virus has been shown to induce a form of “immune amnesia,” whereby measles virus eliminates as many as half of the antibodies generated against other infectious agents to which an individual was exposed previously. Thus, persons who survive measles infection may become vulnerable once again to a range of other diseases, such as chickenpox and polio. By contrast, individuals vaccinated against measles do not experience a loss of immunity to other infectious agents. Deaths attributed to measles usually result from secondary bronchopneumonia caused by bacterial organisms entering the inflamed bronchial tree. Complications of measles are frequent and include a superimposed bacterial ear infection or pneumonia or a primary measles lung infection. Encephalitis is a rare occurrence. Measles virus can invade various organ systems and cause hepatitis, appendicitis, and gangrene of the extremities. A large percentage of cases of severe measles are associated with inadequate intake of vitamin A, and there is evidence that treatment with vitamin A may reduce measles complications. On very rare occasions, persistent infection with a mutant measles virus can cause a degenerative central nervous system disease called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), in which there is a gradual onset of progressive behavioral and intellectual deterioration. Motor incoordination and impairment of speech and sight subsequently develop. The final stages of stupor, dementia, blindness, and death occur within six to nine months. There is no treatment for SSPE. Measles vaccine and eradication efforts How We Develop New VaccinesWorried about diphtheria? No? Thank vaccines.(more) See all videos for this article Mortality caused by measles declined steadily in the 20th century as the health of children and infants improved and effective treatment of complications became possible through the use of sulfonamide and antibiotic drugs. The widespread use of measles vaccine, beginning in the late 1960s, raised hopes for the eventual eradication of the disease. In the following decades, however, measles remained a leading cause of childhood mortality worldwide, primarily because attack rates remained high in less-developed countries, where factors such as malnutrition and weak public health infrastructure challenged infant health and the establishment of immunization programs. In the early 21st century, campaigns were initiated to increase vaccination, particularly in less-developed countries. International efforts led to a significant reduction in measles cases and deaths, bringing global elimination of the disease within reach. At the same time, supporting this progress, many wealthier countries, including the United States and some countries in the European Union, had successfully eradicated measles. Explore Britannica Premium! The trusted destination for professionals, college students, and lifelong learners. SUBSCRIBE In the second decade of the 21st century, however, large measles outbreaks continued to occur in countries with low vaccination rates. In late 2019 in Samoa, for instance, a severe outbreak of measles resulted in school closures and government shutdown, followed by a mass vaccination campaign to prevent further spread of the disease. Measles also reemerged in several countries where it previously had been eradicated, a trend attributed to alarming declines in vaccination coverage. Of particular concern was the return of measles in developed countries such as the United States, where the disease had been eliminated by 2000, and the United Kingdom. In these countries, measles reemerged in the form of small outbreaks that were typically concentrated in areas with relatively high proportions of unvaccinated individuals. In early 2025, the death of a child amid a measles outbreak in Texas marked the country’s first death caused by measles in more than a decade. Coincident with the return of measles in more developed countries, however, was a massive surge in the disease in countries with traditionally low vaccination coverage, resulting in large increases in measles cases worldwide. Global incidence was notably high in 2019, with more than 364,800 cases reported between June and July alone that year—far exceeding the number of cases reported worldwide over that time frame for any year since 2006, according to officials with the World Health Organization (WHO). The largest increases occurred in countries in Africa, Europe, and the Western Pacific. By early 2020 the Democratic Republic of the Congo was in the midst of a severe measles epidemic, with more than 6,000 deaths according to WHO. WHO and health officials in regions affected by measles outbreaks increased efforts to bolster vaccination rates to stop the disease from spreading further. Find out from Dr. Tina Tan of Northwestern University why adult vaccination against various types of diseases is importantLearn about the importance of adult vaccination against measles and other diseases.(more) See all videos for this article Measles vaccines are live vaccines that work against measles alone or in combination against other agents, specifically with rubella (MR), mumps and rubella (MMR), or mumps, rubella, and varicella (MMRV). The vaccines typically are given in two doses. In the United States, for example, the first dose is given at 12 to 15 months of age, and the second dose is recommended at four to six years. In other countries, the vaccine is given first at nine months and the second dose later. The second dose of MMR must be given at least four weeks after the first dose; in adults whose vaccination status is uncertain, the two doses typically are given four weeks apart. The youngest age at which the vaccines can be given is six months, though revaccination (with two doses) is needed later. See MMR vaccine. Similar illnesses Measles must be differentiated from other disorders accompanied by an eruption. In roseola infantum, a disease seen in babies, a measleslike rash appears after the child has had a high temperature for two or three days, but there is no fever at the time of the rash. German measles (rubella) can be superficially differentiated from measles by the shorter course of the disease and mildness of the symptoms. Sometimes the rashes of scarlet fever, serum reactions, and other conditions may, on certain parts of the body, look like measles. Drugs that may produce rashes similar to measles are phenobarbital, diphenylhydantoin, the sulfonamides, phenolphthalein, and penicillin. Britannica Editors Last updated Aug. 28, 2026 •History Britannica AI Ask Anything Table of Contents Table of Contents Ask Anything Top Questions What is a vaccine? A vaccine is a suspension of weakened, killed, or fragmented microorganisms or toxins or other biological preparation, such as those consisting of antibodies, lymphocytes, or mRNA, that is administered primarily to prevent disease. How are vaccines made? A vaccine is produced by first generating the antigen—the component that stimulates the immune system to respond. This antigen can take several forms, including an inactivated virus or bacterium, a purified subunit of the pathogen, or a recombinant protein engineered from it. Once the antigen is produced, it is carefully isolated and purified. Additional ingredients are then incorporated to boost its effectiveness and maintain stability during storage. Finally, the vaccine is manufactured on a large scale and packaged for widespread distribution. What is a vaccine delivery system? A vaccine delivery system refers to the method used to package and administer the immune-stimulating component of a vaccine so that it reaches the appropriate tissue in the body. Common examples include liposomes, emulsions, and microparticles. How do vaccines work? A vaccine works by mimicking an infection, prompting the body to generate antibodies against a specific pathogen. Later, if the vaccinated individual is exposed to that pathogen, the immune system recognizes it quickly and mounts a defense, preventing illness. Measles outbreak has sickened 676 in Pa., including four fully vaccinated people

Measles outbreak has sickened 676 in Pa., including four fully vaccinated people
新闻简报A measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has sickened 676 people, including 4 fully vaccinated individuals. The outbreak, centered in Lancaster County since April, has resulted in 124 hospitalizations and 2 deaths. Health officials are deploying vaccination clinics and addressing misinformation about the outbreak.
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026-09-11 to 2026-09-11.
地点与对象
美国宾夕法尼亚州,676人受影响,37个县有病例报告。
事件
确认的 暴发 麻疹 病例共 676 例,其中包括 4 名完成疫苗接种的个体。

州卫生官员正在通过疫苗接种诊所应对,处理错误信息,并调查死亡事件。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles outbreak has sickened 676 in Pa., including four fully vaccinated people

News Brief
A measles outbreak in Pennsylvania has sickened 676 people, including 4 fully vaccinated individuals. The outbreak, centered in Lancaster County since April, has resulted in 124 hospitalizations and 2 deaths. Health officials are deploying vaccination clinics and addressing misinformation about the outbreak.
Original source text (English)
State health officials have recorded cases of measles in 37 of the commonwealth's 67 counties. Photo by Getty Images Plus) The commonwealth's top doctor stressed the best protection against measles is to get fully vaccinated. During an update on the measles outbreak, Pennsylvania Health Secretary Dr. Debra L. Bogen said it has now sickened 676 people in the commonwealth. Additionally, she said state health officials have recorded cases of measles in 37 of the commonwealth's 67 counties. They included two deaths associated with the virus, announced last month , and four cases among people fully vaccinated for their ages. The Measles, Mumps and Rubella (MMR) vaccine is recommended for children 12 months and older. "It's important to know that people who are vaccinated rarely get measles because the two-dose MMR vaccine offers 97% protection against measles. That means for every 100 people who receive both doses, about three individuals may develop a mild case of measles if they're exposed," Bogen said. SUBSCRIBE: GET THE MORNING HEADLINES DELIVERED TO YOUR INBOX. The four infected despite being vaccinated are residents of Center, Jefferson, Lancaster, and Lycoming counties, she said. Measles is a highly contagious disease that spreads through infected people breathing, coughing or sneezing. Individuals with measles develop high fevers, coughs and red, watery eyes. That's followed by a characteristic rash starting at the head three to five days after symptoms begin. About 20% of cases require hospitalization. Among the cases reported in Pennsylvania, 124 patients have been hospitalized, according to health officials. Pennsylvania has been responding to a measles outbreak centered on Lancaster County since April. Late last month, the Health Department announced it had recorded the first measles-related deaths in the nation this year and the first in decades for Pennsylvania. The announcement became a flashpoint in the national debate over vaccines. U.S. Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. questioned the accuracy of the report, delivered by Bogen and Gov. Josh Shapiro. Lancaster County Republican elected officials joined Kennedy, claiming the report was inaccurate. Photo of a single dose of the MMR vaccine. (Joshua Haiar/South Dakota Searchlight) Kennedy has long been a skeptic on vaccine efficacy, linking shots to autism without evidence. Lancaster County Coroner Stephen Diamantoni earlier this week released additional information about the deaths. While both infants were infected with measles, one died of a ruptured spleen and the other had a genetic condition that made her highly vulnerable to infection and the measles killed her. Bogen addressed inquiries about why the Health Department has not released more information about the deaths. She said the department's work is governed by the state Vital Statistics Law and the Disease Prevention and Control Law, which allow personal information to be released only under narrow conditions that don't apply in the Lancaster County deaths. "Unlike the Department of Health, county coroners and healthcare providers have different laws and regulations that govern their work and what they can disclose. So there may be differences in the level of detail made available," Bogen said. Bogen said the state Health Department and its partners are responding to the outbreak by deploying resources and personnel and the MMR vaccine. The vaccination rate for Pennsylvania kindergarteners is 93%, she added. "We are not waiting for Pennsylvanians to come to us. We are making the vaccine accessible to anyone who wants it," Bogen said, noting state health workers have held more than 120 pop-up MMR vaccination clinics at churches, fire halls, community centers and private homes. "Dozens of additional pop-up clinics are already scheduled for the upcoming weeks and months, tailored directly to community interest and need," she said. Anyone interested in hosting a MMR vaccination event can call the Health Department at 877-724-3258. The MMR vaccine is also available at each of the department's 59 state health centers, which are listed at health.pa.gov/shc . The state provides the latest information about the measles outbreak at pa.gov/measles . Suspected measles cases kill nearly 1,000 as Bangladesh struggles to contain outbreak - NBC News

Suspected measles cases kill nearly 1,000 as Bangladesh struggles to contain outbreak - NBC News

Suspected measles cases kill nearly 1,000 as Bangladesh struggles to contain outbreak - NBC News

News Brief
Bangladesh is grappling with a severe measles outbreak that has killed nearly 1,000 children since the start of the year. The outbreak, which has affected over 166,000 children, has overwhelmed hospitals and continues to spread. The government has launched an emergency vaccination drive since March, but the death toll is rising daily. The situation highlights the challenges faced by Bangladesh in maintaining its vaccination programs, particularly in the wake of disruptions caused by the pandemic and political upheaval.
Original source text (English)

She is one of thousands of children sickened by a measles outbreak that is sweeping across the South Asian country and has killed hundreds of children following setbacks to the national vaccine program. Ramisa’s mother, Ranu Akhter, traveled hundreds of miles after doctors in eastern Bangladesh referred her to a specialized child hospital in Dhaka to seek treatment for the life-threatening condition. “Fourteen days ago, when we were at the district hospital, there were other children being treated alongside my daughter. I saw that those children also had red rashes on their bodies, sores in their mouths and fevers,” Akhter told The Associated Press at Dhaka Shishu Hospital. “It made me realize how widespread the measles outbreak is,” she said. Every day, the death toll is rising. 0 seconds of 15 secondsVolume 90% Press shift question mark to access a list of keyboard shortcuts Keyboard ShortcutsEnabledDisabled Play/PauseSPACE Increase Volume↑ Decrease Volume↓ Seek Forward→ Seek Backward← Captions On/Offc Fullscreen/Exit Fullscreenf Mute/Unmutem Decrease Caption Size- Increase Caption Size+ or = Seek %0-9 Play 0.5x1x1.25x1.5x2x Live 00:00 00:15 00:15 00:15 Thousands of measles cases confirmed in the U.S. 00:0000:00 Bangladesh has been racing to halt the outbreak’s spread since March, when more than 100 children died in less than a month. By Tuesday, the death toll from suspected measles cases reached 999 since the start of the year, according to the government’s Directorate General of Health Services. Since March, the country has recorded more than 166,000 suspected measles cases, including 19,835 laboratory-confirmed infections, according to the Ministry of Health. The government launched an emergency vaccination drive in March together with the World Health Organization, the U.N. children’s agency and the Gavi vaccine alliance. It began with a focus on children from 6 months to 5 years old and later expanded nationwide in phases. Measles is a highly contagious airborne disease that causes fever, respiratory symptoms and a characteristic rash. It can sometimes have severe or fatal complications, especially in young children, according to the WHO. Two doses of a vaccine provide strong protection against measles, but the WHO says 95% of the population has to be vaccinated in order to stop the disease from spreading — which protects children like Ramisa who are too young to be vaccinated as well as immunocompromised people. About 86% of children in Bangladesh got their recommended second dose of measles vaccine last year, according to an estimate by the World Health Organization and UNICEF. That’s down from 93% in 2024. A few years ago, Bangladesh had measles largely under control, with a vaccination program that was credited for decades for protecting children and mothers from the deadly diseases of tuberculosis, diphtheria, whooping cough, tetanus, poliomyelitis and measles. From 2021 to 2025, the country recorded just 100 to 300 cases per year, down from 2,410 cases in 2020. But in the last few years, disruptions to the vaccine program have left a growing number of children unvaccinated. First the Covid-19 pandemic interrupted routine vaccinations, and then in 2024 the government largely missed a vaccination campaign previously carried out every four years. Atiqul Islam, a neonatal and child specialist at Bangladesh Shishu Hospital and Institute, said that during the pandemic period in 2021 and 2022, many parents did not take their children to get vaccinated. “So, if you look at children who are now 4 or 5 years old, many of them missed their vaccinations. That created a gap. During the previous interim government, vaccines were also unavailable at many vaccination centers,” he said. Bangladesh’s current government, headed by Prime Minister Tarique Rahman, said the failures in planning under ousted Prime Minister Sheikh Hasina and interim leader Muhammad Yunus left the country with shortages of vaccines and far behind on coverage. The 2024 measles vaccination campaign was disrupted during the political upheaval that led to Hasina’s ouster. She left the country in the face of a mass uprising in August 2024, and was followed by Yunus, who led an interim administration that transferred power to an elected government after an election in February. From exile in India, Hasina blamed the Yunus-led government for the disaster. At Bangladesh Shishu Hospital, Islam said the situation has improved, but not enough. The doctor said his colleagues are struggling to treat infected children who often arrive at the hospital with serious complications such as pneumonia, diarrhea and encephalitis, or brain swelling. “For the past six months, we have been treating measles patients continuously, and we are exhausted,” he said. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Whatever happened to the measles outbreak that shocked a country this year? - NPR

News Brief
Bangladesh continues to grapple with a measles outbreak, with over 50,000 confirmed or suspected cases and more than 1,000 child deaths. Despite a nationwide vaccination campaign that vaccinated 18.4 million children, the outbreak persists due to immunization gaps and malnutrition. Authorities are planning to vaccinate an additional 1.6 million children and procure 2.5 million more doses, but the situation remains critical.
Original source text (English)

Fresh Air Wild Card with Rachel Martin It's Been a Minute Embedded Get NPR+ More Podcasts & Shows Search Newsletters NPR Shop Get NPR+ Sign InPersonalize Your Experience Tiny Desk New Music Friday All Songs Considered Music Features Live Sessions About NPR Diversity Support Careers Press Ethics Has Bangladesh brought its measles crisis under control? Bangladesh has seen over 50,000 confirmed or suspected cases of measles this year. The death toll among children just surpassed 1,000. How are officials trying to bring the outbreak under control? Special Series Following Up Whatever happened to the measles outbreak that shocked a country this year? September 9, 20269:04 AM ET By Ali Asif Shawon The measles ward at Dhaka Shishu Hospital is full, even five months after the start of a massive outbreak in Bangladesh. Ali Asif Shawon for NPR hide caption toggle caption Ali Asif Shawon for NPR In May, we posted a story about the rapid and devastating spread of measles in Bangladesh. The headline: More than 500 children have died in an outbreak that the world is virtually ignoring. We asked the reporter who covered the story to tell us what has transpired since then. About This Series Over the next week, we'll be looking back at some of our past global health and development stories to see "whatever happened to ..." There was optimism early on about bringing the measles outbreak in Bangladesh under control. Global Health More than 500 children have died in an outbreak that the world is virtually ignoring In April, the country's health minister, Sardar Md. Sakhawat Husain, offered this assessment: "Measles outbreak has somewhat decreased in the country. Once the nationwide vaccination campaign begins on April 20, the situation will improve further." The goal was to vaccinate 18 million children ages 6 months to 5 years, even if records indicated they'd already been vaccinated. That goal was met and then some, with 18.4 million vaccinations. Five months later, the outbreak continues apace. In August, the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention reported that Bangladesh has had more measles cases than any other country from January to June of this year: 57,561. On the day of Aug. 31, authorities recorded 1,162 suspected cases and 94 confirmed cases in 24 hours. On September 9, the toll for children who have died from suspected or confirmed measles cases since March 15 passed 1,000, according to the Directorate General of Health Services of Bangladesh. Why has the virus not been conquered? It turns out that 18 million vaccinations wasn't enough to reach all the unvaccinated children. Health experts in the country now say that 22.6 million is the true target. This persistent immunization gap has given the highly contagious virus a continued chance to spread. "Many children have still not received the measles vaccine, so we cannot go into a relaxed mode," Tajul Abdul Bari told NPR. He is a vaccine expert and former program manager of the government's Expanded Programme on Immunization. "We need to start a new vaccination campaign, potentially covering children from 6 months up to under 10 years of age. We did something similar in 2014." Dr. Javid Abdelmoneim, president of Médecins Sans Frontières International, told NPR, "The management of a measles outbreak is vaccinating. Vaccinate, vaccinate, vaccinate." "Measles has a very specific biology. It is highly transmissible. One case of measles can affect 100 others," he added. Dr Jahangir Alam, the World Health Organization's divisional coordinator for the capital city of Dhaka, said: "Even small gaps in immunization coverage can trigger outbreaks. Every missed child increases the risk." Indeed, a UNICEF situation report says that 72% of cases are among those with no doses of the two-dose vaccine and 16% of the patients are partially vaccinated. The toll has been worsened because of the country's widespread malnutrition, which increases the risk of severe illness and death from a measles infection. "Children who are underweight or have two or more medical conditions at the same time suffer more, and their condition worsens," says Colonel Latifa Rahman, director of Mohakhali DNCC Hospital, the hospital in the capital that is now doing nothing but measles care. Malnutrition can also lessen the immunity conferred by a vaccine. Next steps On Aug. 18, Bangladesh announced it will vaccinate another 1.6 million children against measles. Former Health Services Division Secretary Md Kamruzzaman Chowdhury said official data shows that in some pockets of the country, there are children between 6 months and 5 years who missed out on vaccination. in pockets of the country. "In light of that, we will administer the 1.6 million vaccines we currently have," he said. The government is also moving to procure another 2.5 million doses to guard against future shortages, according to Kamruzzaman. "We will give 1.6 million now and are procuring 2.5 million vaccines for the future. We have spoken with UNICEF about purchasing these vaccines," he added. Six-month-old Sumaiya Saima needs oxygen support due to measles complications. The infant is in the measles ward at Shishu Hospital of Dhaka. Ali Asif Shawon for NPR hide caption toggle caption Ali Asif Shawon for NPR So the early forecast of a quick end to the outbreak has been tempered by the reality in Bangladesh. "There is no scope for unrealistic assurances. Even if the pace of vaccination were doubled, it would still take another two to three months to bring the measles outbreak fully under control," State Minister for Health Dr. M.A. Muhit said on Aug. 19. The toll on families As the outbreak continues, thousands of families are being driven into financial distress. Nearly 9 in 10 affected households have borrowed money for treatment and 6 in 10 have exhausted their savings, according to a joint assessment in May and June by the Bangladesh Red Crescent Society and the International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies (IFRC). The assessment covered 2,746 measles-affected households receiving care at 12 government medical colleges and district hospitals. Sonakshi Dey of the IFRC in Bangladesh said economic hardships continue even if a child has recovered: "Beyond direct medical expenses, caregivers often lose income when they need to stay home to care for a sick child or travel repeatedly to health facilities. For low-income households that depend on daily wages, even a short interruption to work can have serious consequences for household wellbeing and food security." Families who have lost a child face the heaviest toll of all. Toriqul Islam, whose 10-month-old boy died due to measles on May 9, said in a heavy tone: "When fate turns against you, this is what happens. I don't know if there is any greater pain in this world. "Let the government do whatever they want, but all I want is for no one to die." Ali Asif Shawon is the chief reporter at Dhaka Tribune in Bangladesh, where he covers politics, security, climate change, migration and global health. He can be reached on LinkedIn. For stories about life in our changing world, subscribe to NPR's Global Health newsletter. NPR does not offer or accept money for coverage or interviews. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Pennsylvania's measles outbreak grows to 624 cases - 6abc Philadelphia

News Brief
As of September 9, 2026, Pennsylvania has reported 624 measles cases across 37 counties, with an additional 91 cases in the past seven days. The outbreak has led to 109 hospitalizations, highlighting its significant public health impact. Health officials are monitoring the situation and have increased vaccination efforts, administering 46,000 doses in August and 6,200 at state-run pop-up clinics. The outbreak is ongoing, and the response remains active with no confirmed resolution. The measles outbreak is a primary concern for public health authorities, as it underscores the need for continued vigilance and vaccination campaigns.
Original source text (English)

By6abc Digital Staff Wednesday, September 9, 2026 9:45PM Share Follow 6abc on Google HARRISBURG, Pa. (WPVI) -- Pennsylvania's measles outbreak continues to grow, with health officials reporting an additional 91 new cases in the past seven days. According to the health department, a total of 624 measles cases have been reported statewide across 37 counties as of Wednesday. The outbreak has resulted in 109 hospitalizations. Health officials also reported an increase in vaccination efforts. Doctors administered 46,000 vaccinations in August, while an additional 6,200 vaccinations were given at state-run pop-up clinics. OffOn Stream on US measles cases surpass 2,000 for the 2nd year in a row: CDC So far this year, 2,030 cases have been recorded in 38 states and D.C. ByMary Kekatos and Georgia Davis June 5, 2026, 6:07 PM Share Measles cases in the United States have surpassed 2,000 for the second year in a row, according to data updated Friday from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). So far in 2026, 2,030 confirmed cases have been recorded in 38 states and the District of Columbia, CDC data shows. Cases have been confirmed in: Alaska, Arizona, California, Colorado, Florida, Georgia, Idaho, Illinois, Kansas, Kentucky, Louisiana, Maine, Maryland, Massachusetts, Michigan, Minnesota, Missouri, Montana, Nebraska, New Jersey, New Mexico, New York, North Carolina, North Dakota, Ohio, Oklahoma, Oregon, Pennsylvania, Rhode Island, South Carolina, South Dakota, Texas, Utah, Vermont, Virginia, Washington, Wisconsin and Wyoming. Related 1 year since measles cases found in Texas, US still seeing surge.
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至2026年,CDC数据显示,今年已有2030例确诊病例记录在38个州和哥伦比亚特区。
地点与对象
此前,美国麻疹病例从未超过2000例。
事件
CDC数据显示,约92%的病例发生在未接种疫苗或疫苗接种状态未知的人群中。
规模、影响与风险
美国麻疹消除状态的审查将于2026年11月由泛美卫生组织进行。
应对、状态与不确定性
目前尚不清楚1月确认的病例情况。
